--- grammar ---

meaning: Expresses an intention or obligation to oneself, meaning 'I should do...' or 'I'll have to...'

notes: Uses native Korean or Sino-Korean numbers depending on the counter. Minutes use Sino-Korean numbers (팔분 - pal-bun).
--- word ---
term: 30초에서
type: noun + particle
breakdown: 30초 (samsip-cho, seconds) + 에서 (eseo, from)
meaning: from 30 seconds
notes: 에서 acts as a time or spatial marker meaning 'from'.
--- word ---
term: 9분을
type: noun + particle
breakdown: 9분 (gu-bun, 9 minutes) + 을 (eul, object marker)
meaning: 9 minutes
notes: 을 is used after a consonant-ending noun.
--- word ---
term: 조리하래
type: verb ending (indirect quotation)
breakdown: 조리하다 (jorihada, to cook) + -라고 해 (-rago hae, they say to) contracted to -래
meaning: it says to cook / they tell me to cook
notes: Contraction of -라고 해요 (-rago haeyo), used to relay instructions, commands, or what someone else said. Casual/spoken form.
--- grammar ---
pattern: --하래요 (-haraeyo) / -래 (-rae)
meaning: Indirect quotation used to relay a command or instruction given by someone else or written instructions.
example: 내일 오래요. (They said to come tomorrow.)
level: Intermediate

=== [12:35] 음흠 ===
meaning: Mhm.
tone: Casual, agreeable humming sound.
--- word ---
term: 음흠
type: interjection
breakdown: 
meaning: mhm / uh-huh
notes: An informal vocalization expressing agreement or acknowledgment.

=== [12:37] 곤란하구먼 ===
meaning: This is a bit tricky/troublesome.
tone: Casual, slightly playful archaic or old-fashioned tone.
--- word ---
term: 곤란하구먼
type: adjective ending
breakdown: 곤란하다 (golranhada, to be difficult/troublesome) + -구먼 (-gumyeon/-guman, ending for realization or reflection)
meaning: it's troublesome, I'm in a bind
notes: The ending -구먼 adds a colloquial, somewhat folk or folksy flavor, often used by older speakers or playfully by younger people to express sudden realization.
--- grammar ---
pattern: -구먼 (-guman) / -구려 (-guryeo)
meaning: Retrospective or reflective sentence ending expressing realization about a current state, often sounding rustic or elderly.
example: 날씨가 참 좋구먼. (The weather is really nice, you know.)
level: Advanced

breakdown: 
meaning: super / best / really
notes: Slang prefix meaning 'the best' or 'super'. Very common in casual speech.

meaning: Quotation form used to express surprise or check information someone else just provided.

=== [12:44] 그래? ===
meaning: Really?
tone: Casual, inquiring.
--- word ---
term: 그래?
type: verb
breakdown: 그렇다 (geureota, to be so) + -어 (-eo, informal ending)
meaning: Is that so? / Really?
notes: Very common casual reaction word meaning 'Is that right?' or 'Really?'.

=== [12:47] 그럼 먹어보긴 해야 될 것 같긴 한데 ===
meaning: Then I suppose I should at least try eating it, though...
tone: Casual, hesitant, pondering out loud.
--- word ---
term: 그럼
type: adverb
breakdown: 그러면 (geureomyeon, if so) contracted
meaning: then / if so
notes: Contraction of 그러면. Used frequently to transition thoughts.
--- word ---
term: 먹어보긴
type: verb + particle
breakdown: 먹다 (meokda) + -어 보다 (try doing) + -기 (nominalizer) + -는 (topic marker)
meaning: as for trying to eat it
notes: Combines -아/어 보다 (to try doing) with the contrasting topic marker -는, implying 'at least try it (even if unsure)'.
--- word ---
term: 해야
type: verb conjugation
breakdown: 하다 (hada, to do) + -아야 하다 (must do)
meaning: have to do
notes: Shortened from 해야 돼.
--- word ---
term: 될
type: verb modifier
breakdown: 되다 (doeda, to become/work out) + -ㄹ (future/modifier suffix)
meaning: that will work / that needs to be
notes: Part of the auxiliary construction -는 것 같다.
--- word ---
term: 것
type: bound noun
breakdown: 
meaning: thing / fact
notes: Used with modifiers to form relative clauses.
--- word ---
term: 같긴
type: adjective + particle
breakdown: 같다 (gatda, to seem) + -기는 (particle for concession)
meaning: it seems like, though
notes: -기는 acknowledges a point while hinting at hesitation or a counterpoint.
--- word ---
term: 한데
type: conjunction / connective ending
breakdown: 
meaning: but / and
notes: Used to soften a statement or trail off, indicating a contrasting thought follows.
--- grammar ---
pattern: -는 것 같긴 한데 (-neun geot gatgin hande)
meaning: Used to express a tentative opinion or hesitation, meaning 'it seems like... but...'
example: 비가 올 것 같긴 한데 잘 모르겠어요. (It seems like it might rain, but I'm not sure.)
level: Intermediate

=== [13:01] 근데 이건 에어프라이어에 ===

term: 근데
type: conjunction / adverb
breakdown: 그런데

meaning: this (topic)

meaning: in/to the air fryer

=== [13:04] 에어프라이어는 없는데? ===
meaning: I don't have an air fryer, though?

=== [13:15] 아 그럼 이거를 에어프라이어에 돌릴까? ===
meaning: Ah, then should I pop this into the air fryer?
tone: Casual, deliberating, conversational.
--- word ---
term: 아
type: interjection
breakdown: 
meaning: ah
notes: Exclamation indicating sudden realization.
--- word ---
term: 그럼
type: conjunction
breakdown: 그러면
meaning: then / in that case
notes: Contraction of 그러면 (if so / then).
--- word ---
term: 이거를
type: pronoun + particle
breakdown: 이거 + 를
meaning: this (object)
notes: Object form of 이거 (this). Spoken Korean often uses 이거를, which can also be contracted to 이걸.

term: 에어프라이어에
type: noun + particle
breakdown: 에어프라이어 + 에
meaning: in the air fryer
notes: Locative particle 에 marks the destination or place where an action takes place.
--- word ---
term: 돌릴까?
type: verb ending
breakdown: 돌리다 + ㄹ까
meaning: shall I run / shall I heat it up?
notes: Casual interrogative ending -ㄹ까? used to ponder or ask oneself (or listeners) about a future action. 돌리다 literally means 'to turn/spin', but here means to run an appliance.

pattern: -ㄹ까?
meaning: Shall I...? / Wondering if...
example: 이거 먹을까?

term: F
type: noun (loanword/abbreviation)
breakdown: F
meaning: air fryer
notes: Short for air fryer (에어프라이어). It is very common in Korean casual speech and text to abbreviate English loanwords into a single alphabet letter.
--- word ---
term: 에다가
type: particle
breakdown: 에 + 다가
meaning: in, on, into
notes: A particle attached to nouns indicating the target or location where an action is directed, often implying doing something else on top of or while using that object.
--- word ---
term: 이걸
type: pronoun + particle
breakdown: 이것 + 을
meaning: this (object)
notes: Contraction of 이것을 (this + object particle). In spoken Korean, 이것을 almost always collapses to 이걸.
--- word ---
term: 돌릴까?
type: verb ending
breakdown: 돌리다 + ㄹ까?
meaning: shall I run / shall I heat up?
notes: Informal casual ending used to ask oneself or others about a tentative plan or choice. 돌리다 literally means to turn or spin, but is used for running appliances like microwaves or air fryers.

meaning: Used to wonder about or suggest a future action, equivalent to 'shall I?' or 'I wonder if...'

=== [13:25] 그러면 ===

=== [18:24] 지금 살짝 어지러워 ===
meaning: I'm a little dizzy right now.
tone: Casual, confessional, tipsy state.
--- word ---
term: 지금
type: adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: now
notes: Refers to the present moment. Compare with 이제 (ije, 'now/from now on'), which often marks a transition to a new action or state, whereas 지금 strictly means 'at this very moment'.
--- word ---
term: 살짝
type: adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: slightly, a little
notes: Colloquial adverb used to describe a minor degree of something. Very common in everyday speech instead of slightly more formal terms like 조금 (jogeum).
--- word ---
term: 어지러워
type: adjective
breakdown: 어지럽다 + 어/아
meaning: dizzy
notes: Base form is 어지럽다 (to be dizzy). This is a ㅂ-irregular verb, meaning when a vowel-starting suffix follows, the ㅂ drops and changes to 우 or 오 (어지럽 + 어 -> 어지러워). In context, she is getting tipsy from drinking beer.
--- grammar ---
pattern: ㅂ irregular verbs
meaning: Verbs ending in ㅂ drop the ㅂ and add 우 or 오 before vowel endings.
example: 어지럽다 -> 어지러워 (dizzy), 춥다 -> 추워 (cold)
level: Beginner

=== [18:26] 배가 고파서 ===

--- word ---
term: 배가
type: noun + particle
breakdown: 배 + 가
meaning: stomach (subject)
notes: 배 can mean stomach, boat, or pear depending on context. Here it is paired with the subject particle 가.
--- word ---
term: 고파서
type: verb + connective ending
breakdown: 고프다 + 아서/어서
meaning: because (it) is hungry
notes: Base form is 배고프다 (to be hungry), often shortened to 고프다. The connective ending -아서/-어서 indicates cause and effect ('because'). Note that 배고프다 is literally 'stomach is hungry'.

pattern: -아서/어서 (Reason)
meaning: Connects two clauses, showing that the first clause is the cause or reason for the second.
example: 비가 와서 씁쓸해요. (It's bitter because it's raining / context-dependent).

breakdown: 먹다 + 어야 하다 + 겠 + 어

notes: Combination of -어야 하다 (must/have to) and -겠- (conjecture/intention), finished with casual banmal ending -어. Expresses a sudden realization of necessity or personal determination.
--- grammar ---
pattern: -어야겠어
meaning: Expresses the speaker's sudden decision or realization that they must do something.
example: 이제 자야겠어. (I need to go to sleep now.)
level: Intermediate

term: 언니들이랑
type: noun + particle
breakdown: 언니 + 들 (plural) + 이랑 (with)
meaning: with my older sisters
notes: The suffix -들 pluralizes 언니 (older sister for females). The particle -이랑 means 'with' (casual, equivalent to -와/과 or -하고).
--- word ---
term: 이제
type: adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: now, at that time / filler
notes: While literally meaning 'now', native speakers frequently use 이제 as a conversational filler or discourse marker when pausing to recall a memory, similar to 'um' or 'well'.
--- word ---
term: 있었잖아
type: verb + ending
breakdown: 있다 + 었/었 (past) + 잖아
meaning: I was (there), as you know / remember?
notes: The ending -잖아 is used to remind the listener of something they already know or to seek agreement ('you know', 'right?'). Casual register (반말).
--- grammar ---
pattern: -잖아(요)
meaning: As you know... / Don't you remember...?
example: 날씨가 좋잖아 (The weather is nice, as you know)
level: Intermediate

notes: The object marker 을 attaches to nouns ending in a consonant.

term: 세븐일레븐이랑
type: noun + particle
breakdown: 세븐일레븐 (Seven-Eleven) + 이랑 (and)
meaning: Seven-Eleven and
notes: -이랑 means 'and' (connecting nouns) in casual spoken Korean. The formal equivalent is -와/과, and the conversational equivalent with batchim is -과/이랑.
--- word ---
term: 로손만
type: noun + particle
breakdown: 로손 (Lawson) + 만 (only)
meaning: only Lawson
notes: -만 translates to 'only' or 'just'. It replaces particles like -이/가 or -을/를.
--- word ---
term: 갔다
type: verb
breakdown: 가다 (to go) + -았- (past tense) + -다
meaning: went
notes: Base verb 가다 combined with the past tense marker -았-.
--- word ---
term: 와
type: verb ending / auxiliary
breakdown: 오다 (to come) + -아 (imperative/connector)
meaning: come / go and come back
notes: The phrase 다녀오다 or 갔다 오다 literally means 'go and come back', which is the standard way Koreans say 'to make a quick trip to a place and return'.
--- grammar ---
pattern: -고 오다 / -고 가다
meaning: To do something and come/go back; making a quick trip.
example: 편의점에 갔다 와. (Go to the convenience store and come back.)
level: Beginner

type: adverb
breakdown: 진짜 (real/really)
meaning: really / seriously
notes: Extremely common conversational adverb used for emphasis.
--- word ---
term: 저런이다

breakdown: 저런 (such a sight / that kind of thing) + 이다 (to be)
meaning: it's such a sight / that's something
notes: 저런 is an exclamation or demonstrative pronoun expressing 'such a thing' or 'how deplorable/amazing'.
--- word ---
term: 저런
type: interjection / demonstrative
breakdown: 저런 (that kind of)
meaning: my goodness / oh dear / such a sight
notes: Used both as a determiner ('such...') and as an exclamation when witnessing something absurd or cute.

breakdown: 하루 + 에
meaning: per day, a day
notes: 에 indicates frequency or time distribution here (per day).
--- word ---
term: 세
type: determiner
breakdown: 세 (native Korean number three before nouns)
meaning: three
notes: Native Korean number modifier for 3. Changes from 셋 (set) to 세 (se) when directly modifying a noun like 끼 (meals).
--- word ---
term: 끼를
type: noun + particle
breakdown: 끼 + 를
meaning: meals (object)
notes: Counter for meals. 세 끼 means 'three meals'.
--- word ---
term: 넘게
type: adverb / particle
breakdown: 넘다 + 게
meaning: more than
notes: Derived from 넘다 (to exceed/cross) with the adverbial suffix -게. Used after numbers to mean 'over' or 'more than'.
--- word ---
term: 먹게
type: verb ending
breakdown: 먹다 + 게 되다
meaning: to end up eating
notes: Part of the auxiliary pattern -게 되다, which expresses that an action happens naturally or by circumstance rather than direct willpower.
--- word ---
term: 돼
type: verb
breakdown: 되다 + 어
meaning: become / turn out
notes: Combined with 게 되다, forms the completed casual ending -게 돼.
--- grammar ---
pattern: -게 되다
meaning: Expresses an indirect result or a change in state—to end up doing something or happen to do something.
example: 한국에 오게 되었어요 (I ended up coming to Korea)
level: Intermediate

meaning: who was / being
notes: Combines the pronoun 나 (I), the copula 이다 (to be), and the modifier suffix -ㄴ which turns the clause into an adjective modifying the following noun.
--- word ---
term: 거잖아
type: bound noun + copula + ending
breakdown: 것 + 이 + -잖아
meaning: isn't it that... / it's because...
notes: A standard conversational pattern (-잖아) used to remind the listener of something obvious they already know. Translated naturally as 'that's just how I was, right?'
--- grammar ---
pattern: -잖아(요)
meaning: You know that... / Isn't it that... Used to remind someone of a shared fact.
example: 내일은 주말이잖아 (Tomorrow is the weekend, you know).
level: Intermediate

notes: Used before counter nouns or actions to mean 'a' or 'once'.
--- word ---
term: 번
type: bound noun
breakdown: 
meaning: time / instance
notes: Counter for actions or occurrences. 한 번 means 'once'.
--- word ---
term: 해볼까
type: verb
breakdown: 하다 (to do) + 보다 (to try) + ㄹ까 (shall I? ending)
meaning: shall I try doing?
notes: The helper verb -보다 means to try doing something. The ending -ㄹ까? expresses a tentative proposal or self-questioning ('shall I?').
--- grammar ---
pattern: -아/어 볼까?
meaning: Shall I try doing...?
example: 한 번 먹어 볼까? (Shall I try eating it once?)
level: Beginner

notes: The ending -네 expresses sudden realization or mild surprise. 똥 (ddong) is vulgar/informal for feces, here referring to her humorous live stream title.

pattern: -네
meaning: Exclamatory ending used to express direct observation or sudden realization.

notes: 쌤 is a very common colloquial contraction of 선생님 (teacher). Could also playfully reference 'You know what I'm saying?' depending on context.

=== [01:14:11] 똥꿈꿔 ===
meaning: Dream of poop
tone: Playful, humorous, affectionate.
--- word ---
term: 똥꿈꿔
type: verb phrase
breakdown: 똥 (poop) + 꿈 (dream) + 꾸다 (to dream) conjugated to 꿔
meaning: dream of poop
notes: Cultural reference: In Korean tradition, dreaming of feces (똥꿈) is considered an extremely lucky omen symbolizing upcoming wealth and good fortune. Hwasa frequently tells her fans this as a quirky blessing.
